========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I came in because I just... I can’t shut my brain off anymore. It’s exhausting. It usually starts with just one little concern, you know? Like a client not responding to an email. 

Which, honestly, happens all the time right now because we're doing this massive data migration for them, and they absolutely refused to upgrade their legacy servers before we started. I told them it was a terrible idea, I actually laid it all out in a presentation, but nobody listens to the IT manager until everything crashes and then suddenly it's my emergency. 

But anyway, they don't reply, and within minutes I’m sitting at my desk entirely convinced that I’m going to lose the contract. And if I lose that, I'll fall behind on my divorce payments. My ex-wife's lawyer is already breathing down my neck about reassessing my income, which is just ridiculous because she got the house and the better car anyway, and she knows I've had a rough year. But my mind just goes straight to the absolute worst case, and I start obsessing that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. It just spirals out of control from one stupid email. I don't even remember what I was working on before I opened my inbox half the time.

Yeah, it really is. It’s... it's like a pinball machine in my head. My mind just keeps jumping from the contract work straight to my mortgage payments. Which, speaking of the mortgage, my bank just completely overhauled their online portal last weekend and it is an absolute disaster. Yeah, so it goes from the mortgage to suddenly I'm agonizing over whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m just damaged goods at this point. I've been trying to put some feelers out for new roles, but if I get a delayed email from a recruiter—even just by a day—it doesn't just feel like a normal delay. It feels like concrete proof that my whole career is closing down. And recruiters are the worst with communication anyway, they rely so heavily on these automated tracking systems now that they don't even read half the resumes submitted to them, which completely defeats the purpose of having a human HR department if you ask me. But yeah, the worrying just bleeds into everything.

Well, yeah, honestly. I’ve started avoiding things because I just can't handle the stress of it. Like, I’ve missed networking follow-ups recently. And I’ve delayed some client deliverables because I get completely paralyzed anticipating every single thing that could go wrong with the rollout. Back when I started, we were racking physical servers by hand, running cat5 cables through drop ceilings at 2 AM. You had to actually know how the hardware worked, you couldn't just spin up a virtual machine in AWS and call yourself an engineer. Kids coming out of college now, they don't even know what a subnet mask is half the time, they just rely on automated scripts for everything. It's a real problem in the industry.

But... yeah, the avoiding things. It's not just work, either. I used to play in this weekend soccer league, but I’ve totally pulled back from that, and from seeing my friends in general. I just... I’m embarrassed, to be honest. I'm so tense all the time, and I snap at people over nothing. I yelled at a referee a few weeks ago over a completely standard offsides call, which is ridiculous. But I just feel so on edge, I don't even want to be around anyone right now.

Well, I don't really get headaches, but I definitely get... tense. I feel wound up almost all day long. My shoulders get completely locked up, like I'm just physically bracing myself for some terrible news. And I can't sit still. I'll just pace around the house, or if I'm stuck at my desk, I'll be bouncing my leg the entire time I'm on a client call. 

Which, by the way, those calls are getting longer and longer because nobody knows how to write a concise agenda anymore. We used to have ten-minute standups, you know? You'd say what you did, what you're doing, and what's blocking you, and then you get back to work. Now it's these forty-five-minute Zoom meetings where everyone just talks in circles using corporate buzzwords like "synergy" and "bandwidth." And the video quality is always terrible because half of them are working from coffee shops on public Wi-Fi instead of a secure, hardwired connection at home. I actually had to explain to a junior developer last week why doing database migrations over a public network is a massive security risk, and he looked at me like I was speaking Greek. 

But yeah, even when nothing is actually happening on the call, my leg is just bouncing a mile a minute. I just feel so restless all the time.

Sad? I mean, maybe a little down sometimes, but not really hopeless. It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Though, speaking of depression, I was reading this article the other day about how the tech industry has some of the highest burnout rates of any profession right now. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them "sprints," but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to "move fast and break things." Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. 

But, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. Just really, really wound up and stressed about everything falling apart.

of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ve been snapping at people over stupid things, like a friend being late to soccer or my family asking a simple question, and History]\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, honestly. I’ve started avoiding things because I just can't handle the stress of it. Like, I’ve missed networking follow-ups recently. And I’ve delayed some client deliverables because I get completely paralyzed anticipating every single thing that could go wrong with the rollout. Which is so frustrating because I know exactly how to execute these projects. I’ve been doing this for twenty years. Back when I started, we were racking physical servers by hand, running cat5 cables through drop ceilings at 2 AM. You had to actually know how the hardware worked, you couldn't just spin up a virtual machine in AWS and call yourself an engineer. I'll just pace around the house, or if I'm stuck at my desk, I'll be bouncing my leg the entire time I'm on a client call. \n\nWhich, by the way, those calls are getting longer and longer because nobody knows how to write a concise agenda anymore. Now it's these forty-five-minute Zoom meetings where everyone just talks in circles using corporate buzzwords like \"synergy\" and \"bandwidth.\" And the video quality is always terrible because half of them are working from coffee shops on public Wi-Fi instead of a secure, hardwired connection at home. I actually had to explain to a junior developer last week why doing database migrations over a public network is a massive security risk, and he looked at me like I was speaking Greek. \n\nBut yeah, even when nothing is actually happening on the call, my leg is just bouncing a mile a minute. I just feel so restless all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless about your situation?\n\nPatient: Sad? I mean, maybe a little down sometimes, but not really hopeless. It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, sleep is... it's a disaster, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, replaying every single thing I said in a meeting, or worrying about interview answers for jobs I haven't even applied for yet. I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. 

Which is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They complain about getting a text message that they can acknowledge from their bed while they restart a pod in Kubernetes. They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. 

But anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.

Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. 

Which, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. It’s all just people spamming you on LinkedIn with automated scripts. Back in the day, you’d go to a conference, maybe grab a beer, exchange physical business cards, and actually build a relationship. Now I get fifty messages a day from "growth hackers" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.

But it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. 

But I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.

Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. 

Which is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. 

But, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.

It feels like concrete proof that my whole career is closing down. And recruiters are the worst with communication anyway, they rely so heavily on these automated tracking systems now that they don't even read half the resumes submitted to them, which completely defeats the purpose of having a human HR department if you ask me. But yeah, the worrying just bleeds into everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, honestly. I’ve started avoiding things because I just can't handle the stress of it. Like, I’ve missed networking follow-ups recently. And I’ve delayed some client deliverables because I get completely paralyzed anticipating every single thing that could go wrong with the rollout. Which is so frustrating because I know exactly how to execute these projects. I’ve been doing this for twenty years. Back when I started, we were racking physical servers by hand, running cat5 cables through drop ceilings at 2 AM. You had to actually know how the hardware worked, you couldn't just spin up a virtual machine in AWS and call yourself an engineer. Kids coming out of college now, they don't even know what a subnet mask is half the time, they just rely on automated scripts for everything. It's a real problem in the industry.\n\nBut... yeah, the avoiding things. It's not just work, either. I used to play in this weekend soccer league, but I’ve totally pulled back from that, and from seeing my friends in general. I just... I’m embarrassed, to be honest. I'm so tense all the time, and I snap at people over nothing. I yelled at a referee a few weeks ago over a completely standard offsides call, which is ridiculous. But I just feel so on edge, I don't even want to be around anyone right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really get headaches, but I definitely get... tense. I feel wound up almost all day long. My shoulders get completely locked up, like I'm just physically bracing myself for some terrible news. And I can't sit still. I'll just pace around the house, or if I'm stuck at my desk, I'll be bouncing my leg the entire time I'm on a client call. \n\nWhich, by the way, those calls are getting longer and longer because nobody knows how to write a concise agenda anymore. We used to have ten-minute standups, you know? You'd say what you did, what you're doing, and what's blocking you, and then you get back to work. Now it's these forty-five-minute Zoom meetings where everyone just talks in circles using corporate buzzwords like \"synergy\" and \"bandwidth.\" And the video quality is always terrible because half of them are working from coffee shops on public Wi-Fi instead of a secure, hardwired connection at home. I actually had to explain to a junior developer last week why doing database migrations over a public network is a massive security risk, and he looked at me like I was speaking Greek. \n\nBut yeah, even when nothing is actually happening on the call, my leg is just bouncing a mile a minute. I just feel so restless all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless about your situation?\n\nPatient: Sad? I mean, maybe a little down sometimes, but not really hopeless. It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Though, speaking of depression, I was reading this article the other day about how the tech industry has some of the highest burnout rates of any profession right now. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. Just really, really wound up and stressed about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping due to these worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep is... it's a disaster, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, replaying every single thing I said in a meeting, or worrying about interview answers for jobs I haven't even applied for yet. I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. \n\nWhich is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They complain about getting a text message that they can acknowledge from their bed while they restart a pod in Kubernetes. They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. It’s all just people spamming you on LinkedIn with automated scripts. Back in the day, you’d go to a conference, maybe grab a beer, exchange physical business cards, and actually build a relationship. Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"candidates": ["F41.1", "F43.0", "F48.8"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. \n\nWhich is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. 

Although, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive "Scaled Agile Framework" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. 

But, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.

It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. 

I mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. 

But, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.

We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. It’s all just people spamming you on LinkedIn with automated scripts. Back in the day, you’d go to a conference, maybe grab a beer, exchange physical business cards, and actually build a relationship. Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I mean, work-wise, I’ve been missing networking follow-ups and I've even delayed a few client deliverables lately. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. 

Which, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s just a room full of twenty-something sales reps in branded Patagonia vests trying to sell you some AI-driven cloud security platform that they don't even understand. You ask them one basic technical question about their API endpoints and they just freeze and say they'll "circle back" with their technical lead. It's a total waste of time. I don't even know why they bother hosting these events anymore if they aren't going to send people who actually know the architecture. It's all just marketing fluff.

But, anyway... what were you asking? Oh, activities I enjoy. Yeah, it's not just work. I’ve completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I haven't been hanging out with my friends much either. I’m just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm kind of embarrassed by how I act around them now. I just snap at people over nothing. 

Though, to be fair, the refs in that soccer league have been absolutely blind this season. The last time I actually played, this guy on the other team was throwing elbows the entire first half, and the ref just stood there checking his watch. We pay a small fortune in league fees every season, and they can't even get someone who knows the offside rule. It’s ridiculous, you know? You go out there to blow off some steam and you just end up more frustrated than when you arrived. 

So, um... yeah. I guess I'm just not doing much of anything I used to like doing right now.

I just feel so restless all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless about your situation?\n\nPatient: Sad? I mean, maybe a little down sometimes, but not really hopeless. It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Though, speaking of depression, I was reading this article the other day about how the tech industry has some of the highest burnout rates of any profession right now. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. Just really, really wound up and stressed about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping due to these worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep is... it's a disaster, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, replaying every single thing I said in a meeting, or worrying about interview answers for jobs I haven't even applied for yet. I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. \n\nWhich is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They complain about getting a text message that they can acknowledge from their bed while they restart a pod in Kubernetes. They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. It’s all just people spamming you on LinkedIn with automated scripts. Back in the day, you’d go to a conference, maybe grab a beer, exchange physical business cards, and actually build a relationship. Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like because of these worries and anxieties?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s... it’s really just constant. It’s not one specific thing setting it off, it’s everything all at once. My mind just keeps jumping from contract work to mortgage payments to whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m damaged goods. Like a client not responding to an email. \n\nWhich, honestly, happens all the time right now because we're doing this massive data migration for them, and they absolutely refused to upgrade their legacy servers before we started. I told them it was a terrible idea, I actually laid it all out in a presentation, but nobody listens to the IT manager until everything crashes and then suddenly it's my emergency. \n\nBut anyway, they don't reply, and within minutes I’m sitting at my desk entirely convinced that I’m going to lose the contract. And if I lose that, I'll fall behind on my divorce payments. My ex-wife's lawyer is already breathing down my neck about reassessing my income, which is just ridiculous because she got the house and the better car anyway, and she knows I've had a rough year. But my mind just goes straight to the absolute worst case, and I start obsessing that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. It just spirals out of control from one stupid email. I don't even remember what I was working on before I opened my inbox half the time.\n\nDoctor: Is this kind of worrying happening in other areas of your life besides work and your divorce?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It’s... it's like a pinball machine in my head. My mind just keeps jumping from the contract work straight to my mortgage payments. Which, speaking of the mortgage, my bank just completely overhauled their online portal last weekend and it is an absolute disaster. I don't know who they outsourced their UX design to, but it takes like six clicks just to find the escrow breakdown now. I actually submitted a feedback ticket because it’s just lazy coding, honestly. \n\nBut... sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, the worrying. Yeah, so it goes from the mortgage to suddenly I'm agonizing over whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m just damaged goods at this point. I've been trying to put some feelers out for new roles, but if I get a delayed email from a recruiter—even just by a day—it doesn't just feel like a normal delay. It feels like concrete proof that my whole career is closing down. And recruiters are the worst with communication anyway, they rely so heavily on these automated tracking systems now that they don't even read half the resumes submitted to them, which completely defeats the purpose of having a human HR department if you ask me. But yeah, the worrying just bleeds into everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain situations or activities because of these worries?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, honestly. I’ve started avoiding things because I just can't handle the stress of it. Like, I’ve missed networking follow-ups recently. And I’ve delayed some client deliverables because I get completely paralyzed anticipating every single thing that could go wrong with the rollout. Which is so frustrating because I know exactly how to execute these projects. I’ve been doing this for twenty years. Back when I started, we were racking physical servers by hand, running cat5 cables through drop ceilings at 2 AM. You had to actually know how the hardware worked, you couldn't just spin up a virtual machine in AWS and call yourself an engineer. Kids coming out of college now, they don't even know what a subnet mask is half the time, they just rely on automated scripts for everything. It's a real problem in the industry.\n\nBut... yeah, the avoiding things. It's not just work, either. I used to play in this weekend soccer league, but I’ve totally pulled back from that, and from seeing my friends in general. I just... I’m embarrassed, to be honest. I'm so tense all the time, and I snap at people over nothing. I yelled at a referee a few weeks ago over a completely standard offsides call, which is ridiculous. But I just feel so on edge, I don't even want to be around anyone right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical symptoms like headaches or tremors when you're worrying?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really get headaches, but I definitely get... tense. I feel wound up almost all day long. My shoulders get completely locked up, like I'm just physically bracing myself for some terrible news. And I can't sit still. I'll just pace around the house, or if I'm stuck at my desk, I'll be bouncing my leg the entire time I'm on a client call. \n\nWhich, by the way, those calls are getting longer and longer because nobody knows how to write a concise agenda anymore. We used to have ten-minute standups, you know? You'd say what you did, what you're doing, and what's blocking you, and then you get back to work. Now it's these forty-five-minute Zoom meetings where everyone just talks in circles using corporate buzzwords like \"synergy\" and \"bandwidth.\" And the video quality is always terrible because half of them are working from coffee shops on public Wi-Fi instead of a secure, hardwired connection at home. I actually had to explain to a junior developer last week why doing database migrations over a public network is a massive security risk, and he looked at me like I was speaking Greek. \n\nBut yeah, even when nothing is actually happening on the call, my leg is just bouncing a mile a minute. I just feel so restless all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless about your situation?\n\nPatient: Sad? I mean, maybe a little down sometimes, but not really hopeless. It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Though, speaking of depression, I was reading this article the other day about how the tech industry has some of the highest burnout rates of any profession right now. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. Just really, really wound up and stressed about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping due to these worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep is... it's a disaster, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, replaying every single thing I said in a meeting, or worrying about interview answers for jobs I haven't even applied for yet. I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. \n\nWhich is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They complain about getting a text message that they can acknowledge from their bed while they restart a pod in Kubernetes. They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. It’s all just people spamming you on LinkedIn with automated scripts. Back in the day, you’d go to a conference, maybe grab a beer, exchange physical business cards, and actually build a relationship. Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like because of these worries and anxieties?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I mean, work-wise, I’ve been missing networking follow-ups and I've even delayed a few client deliverables lately. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s just a room full of twenty-something sales reps in branded Patagonia vests trying to sell you some AI-driven cloud security platform that they don't even understand. You ask them one basic technical question about their API endpoints and they just freeze and say they'll \"circle back\" with their technical lead. It's a total waste of time. I don't even know why they bother hosting these events anymore if they aren't going to send people who actually know the architecture. It's all just marketing fluff.\n\nBut, anyway... what were you asking? Oh, activities I enjoy. Yeah, it's not just work. I’ve completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I haven't been hanging out with my friends much either. I’m just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm kind of embarrassed by how I act around them now. I just snap at people over nothing. \n\nThough, to be fair, the refs in that soccer league have been absolutely blind this season. The last time I actually played, this guy on the other team was throwing elbows the entire first half, and the ref just stood there checking his watch. We pay a small fortune in league fees every season, and they can't even get someone who knows the offside rule. It’s ridiculous, you know? You go out there to blow off some steam and you just end up more frustrated than when you arrived. \n\nSo, um... yeah. I guess I'm just not doing much of anything I used to like doing right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific situations or events that trigger your worrying, or is it more of a constant, general feeling of anxiety?\n\nPatient: It’s... it’s really just constant. It’s not one specific thing setting it off, it’s everything all at once. My mind just keeps jumping from contract work to mortgage payments to whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m damaged goods. Like, if there's just a delayed email from a recruiter, that alone can feel like absolute proof that my whole career is closing down. \n\nWhich, honestly, dealing with tech recruiters right now is a complete joke anyway. They rely entirely on these automated LinkedIn scrapers. You get these messages from some 23-year-old saying, \"Hey, saw your impressive background in HTML, would you be interested in a Principal Cloud Architecture role?\" It’s insulting. They don't even know the difference between Java and JavaScript, but they hold the keys to the hiring process. And the ghosting! They’ll string you along for three weeks, demand you do a take-home coding assessment—which is ridiculous, I manage entire departments, I haven't written a sorting algorithm on a whiteboard since 2002—and then they just disappear into the void. The whole hiring ecosystem is completely broken. It's all run by Applicant Tracking Systems that throw out perfectly good resumes if you don't stuff them with the exact right buzzwords. It's just a massive waste of everyone's time.\n\nBut... sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if there are specific triggers. Yeah, so it's not just one trigger. It's just this constant loop in my head about the mortgage, my reputation, the contracts... it just never shuts off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Well, like I said, it's... it's kind of everywhere. I can't really pin it down to just one area. My mind just keeps jumping from my contract work to making the mortgage payments, to... you know, worrying if people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m just damaged goods at this point. Now it's these forty-five-minute Zoom meetings where everyone just talks in circles using corporate buzzwords like \"synergy\" and \"bandwidth.\" And the video quality is always terrible because half of them are working from coffee shops on public Wi-Fi instead of a secure, hardwired connection at home.
