It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Though, speaking of depression, I was reading this article the other day about how the tech industry has some of the highest burnout rates of any profession right now. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. Just really, really wound up and stressed about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping due to these worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep is... it's a disaster, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, replaying every single thing I said in a meeting, or worrying about interview answers for jobs I haven't even applied for yet. I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. \n\nWhich is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They complain about getting a text message that they can acknowledge from their bed while they restart a pod in Kubernetes. They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. It’s all just people spamming you on LinkedIn with automated scripts. Back in the day, you’d go to a conference, maybe grab a beer, exchange physical business cards, and actually build a relationship. Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I used to go to this local tech meetup, but they moved it to this loud brewery downtown where you can't even hear yourself think, and the parking is an absolute nightmare because they decided to put bike lanes everywhere without actually looking at the traffic data.\n\nBut it's not just work stuff I'm avoiding. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. But honestly, the turf they use on those indoor fields now is terrible for your knees anyway. It's all that recycled rubber tire crumb stuff. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like because of these worries and anxieties?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I mean, work-wise, I’ve been missing networking follow-ups and I've even delayed a few client deliverables lately. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s just a room full of twenty-something sales reps in branded Patagonia vests trying to sell you some AI-driven cloud security platform that they don't even understand. You ask them one basic technical question about their API endpoints and they just freeze and say they'll \"circle back\" with their technical lead. It's a total waste of time. I don't even know why they bother hosting these events anymore if they aren't going to send people who actually know the architecture. It's all just marketing fluff.\n\nBut, anyway... what were you asking? Oh, activities I enjoy. Yeah, it's not just work. I’ve completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I haven't been hanging out with my friends much either. I’m just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm kind of embarrassed by how I act around them now. I just snap at people over nothing. \n\nThough, to be fair, the refs in that soccer league have been absolutely blind this season. The last time I actually played, this guy on the other team was throwing elbows the entire first half, and the ref just stood there checking his watch. We pay a small fortune in league fees every season, and they can't even get someone who knows the offside rule. It’s ridiculous, you know? You go out there to blow off some steam and you just end up more frustrated than when you arrived. \n\nSo, um... yeah. I guess I'm just not doing much of anything I used to like doing right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific situations or events that trigger your worrying, or is it more of a constant, general feeling of anxiety?\n\nPatient: It’s... it’s really just constant. They rely entirely on these automated LinkedIn scrapers. You get these messages from some 23-year-old saying, \"Hey, saw your impressive background in HTML, would you be interested in a Principal Cloud Architecture role?\" It’s insulting. They don't even know the difference between Java and JavaScript, but they hold the keys to the hiring process. And the ghosting! They’ll string you along for three weeks, demand you do a take-home coding assessment—which is ridiculous, I manage entire departments, I haven't written a sorting algorithm on a whiteboard since 2002—and then they just disappear into the void. The whole hiring ecosystem is completely broken. It's all run by Applicant Tracking Systems that throw out perfectly good resumes if you don't stuff them with the exact right buzzwords. It's just a massive waste of everyone's time.\n\nBut... sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if there are specific triggers. Yeah, so it's not just one trigger. It's just this constant loop in my head about the mortgage, my reputation, the contracts... it just never shuts off.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular area of your life where you feel the worrying is most prominent?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's... it's kind of everywhere. They've been doing construction on 495 for what feels like a decade now. You try to get to a simple lunch meeting and you're sitting on the outer loop for 45 minutes just staring at the bumper of a Prius. They keep adding these express lanes, but they charge you like thirty dollars just to go five miles during rush hour. Who is paying for that? I mean, unless you're expensing it to a government contract, it's basically highway robbery. And don't even get me started on the Maryland drivers. They just drift across three lanes without a blinker like they're the only ones on the road. It's like nobody ever taught them basic driver's ed. \n\nBut, um... yeah, to answer your question, the worry isn't just one area. It's the work, the house, my reputation... it's all of it just constantly spinning around in my head.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah, I mean, I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier, but it's definitely hitting both my work and my personal life pretty hard. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like because of these worries and anxieties?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I mean, work-wise, I’ve been missing networking follow-ups and I've even delayed a few client deliverables lately. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s just a room full of twenty-something sales reps in branded Patagonia vests trying to sell you some AI-driven cloud security platform that they don't even understand. You ask them one basic technical question about their API endpoints and they just freeze and say they'll \"circle back\" with their technical lead. It's a total waste of time. I don't even know why they bother hosting these events anymore if they aren't going to send people who actually know the architecture. It's all just marketing fluff.\n\nBut, anyway... what were you asking? Oh, activities I enjoy. Yeah, it's not just work. I’ve completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I haven't been hanging out with my friends much either. I’m just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm kind of embarrassed by how I act around them now. I just snap at people over nothing. \n\nThough, to be fair, the refs in that soccer league have been absolutely blind this season. The last time I actually played, this guy on the other team was throwing elbows the entire first half, and the ref just stood there checking his watch. We pay a small fortune in league fees every season, and they can't even get someone who knows the offside rule. It’s ridiculous, you know? You go out there to blow off some steam and you just end up more frustrated than when you arrived. \n\nSo, um... yeah. I guess I'm just not doing much of anything I used to like doing right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific situations or events that trigger your worrying, or is it more of a constant, general feeling of anxiety?\n\nPatient: It’s... it’s really just constant. It’s not one specific thing setting it off, it’s everything all at once. My mind just keeps jumping from contract work to mortgage payments to whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m damaged goods. Like, if there's just a delayed email from a recruiter, that alone can feel like absolute proof that my whole career is closing down. \n\nWhich, honestly, dealing with tech recruiters right now is a complete joke anyway. They rely entirely on these automated LinkedIn scrapers. They don't even know the difference between Java and JavaScript, but they hold the keys to the hiring process. And the ghosting! They’ll string you along for three weeks, demand you do a take-home coding assessment—which is ridiculous, I manage entire departments, I haven't written a sorting algorithm on a whiteboard since 2002—and then they just disappear into the void. The whole hiring ecosystem is completely broken. It's all run by Applicant Tracking Systems that throw out perfectly good resumes if you don't stuff them with the exact right buzzwords. It's just a massive waste of everyone's time.\n\nBut... sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if there are specific triggers. Yeah, so it's not just one trigger. It's just this constant loop in my head about the mortgage, my reputation, the contracts... it just never shuts off.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular area of your life where you feel the worrying is most prominent?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's... it's kind of everywhere. They've been doing construction on 495 for what feels like a decade now. You try to get to a simple lunch meeting and you're sitting on the outer loop for 45 minutes just staring at the bumper of a Prius. They keep adding these express lanes, but they charge you like thirty dollars just to go five miles during rush hour. Who is paying for that? I mean, unless you're expensing it to a government contract, it's basically highway robbery. And don't even get me started on the Maryland drivers. They just drift across three lanes without a blinker like they're the only ones on the road. It's like nobody ever taught them basic driver's ed. \n\nBut, um... yeah, to answer your question, the worry isn't just one area. It's the work, the house, my reputation... it's all of it just constantly spinning around in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is affecting your ability to perform your job or maintain relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier, but it's definitely hitting both my work and my personal life pretty hard. I'll sit there staring at the screen, anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong before I actually send the files over. \n\nAnd honestly, the tools we have to use to send things out now make everything ten times worse. Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like because of these worries and anxieties?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I mean, work-wise, I’ve been missing networking follow-ups and I've even delayed a few client deliverables lately. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s just a room full of twenty-something sales reps in branded Patagonia vests trying to sell you some AI-driven cloud security platform that they don't even understand. You ask them one basic technical question about their API endpoints and they just freeze and say they'll \"circle back\" with their technical lead. It's a total waste of time. I just snap at people over nothing. \n\nThough, to be fair, the refs in that soccer league have been absolutely blind this season. The last time I actually played, this guy on the other team was throwing elbows the entire first half, and the ref just stood there checking his watch. We pay a small fortune in league fees every season, and they can't even get someone who knows the offside rule. It’s ridiculous, you know? You go out there to blow off some steam and you just end up more frustrated than when you arrived. \n\nSo, um... yeah. I guess I'm just not doing much of anything I used to like doing right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific situations or events that trigger your worrying, or is it more of a constant, general feeling of anxiety?\n\nPatient: It’s... it’s really just constant. It’s not one specific thing setting it off, it’s everything all at once. My mind just keeps jumping from contract work to mortgage payments to whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m damaged goods. Like, if there's just a delayed email from a recruiter, that alone can feel like absolute proof that my whole career is closing down. \n\nWhich, honestly, dealing with tech recruiters right now is a complete joke anyway. They rely entirely on these automated LinkedIn scrapers. You get these messages from some 23-year-old saying, \"Hey, saw your impressive background in HTML, would you be interested in a Principal Cloud Architecture role?\" It’s insulting. They don't even know the difference between Java and JavaScript, but they hold the keys to the hiring process. And the ghosting! They’ll string you along for three weeks, demand you do a take-home coding assessment—which is ridiculous, I manage entire departments, I haven't written a sorting algorithm on a whiteboard since 2002—and then they just disappear into the void. The whole hiring ecosystem is completely broken. It's all run by Applicant Tracking Systems that throw out perfectly good resumes if you don't stuff them with the exact right buzzwords. It's just a massive waste of everyone's time.\n\nBut... sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if there are specific triggers. Yeah, so it's not just one trigger. It's just this constant loop in my head about the mortgage, my reputation, the contracts... it just never shuts off.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular area of your life where you feel the worrying is most prominent?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's... it's kind of everywhere. I can't really pin it down to just one area. My mind just keeps jumping from my contract work to making the mortgage payments, to... you know, worrying if people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m just damaged goods at this point. They've been doing construction on 495 for what feels like a decade now. You try to get to a simple lunch meeting and you're sitting on the outer loop for 45 minutes just staring at the bumper of a Prius. They keep adding these express lanes, but they charge you like thirty dollars just to go five miles during rush hour. Who is paying for that? I mean, unless you're expensing it to a government contract, it's basically highway robbery. And don't even get me started on the Maryland drivers. They just drift across three lanes without a blinker like they're the only ones on the road. It's the work, the house, my reputation... it's all of it just constantly spinning around in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is affecting your ability to perform your job or maintain relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier, but it's definitely hitting both my work and my personal life pretty hard. It's like the developers at Microsoft just assume everyone is running a liquid-cooled supercomputer. I don't want them to see me snapping at everyone over nothing, so it's easier to just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you significant distress or interfering with your ability to function on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... distress is a good word for it. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! Now I get fifty messages a day from \"growth hackers\" wanting fifteen minutes of my time to synergize our pipelines. It’s ridiculous. I read an article about how it gets incredibly hot, and it just radiates heat, plus those little black rubber pellets get everywhere in your car and your washing machine. And the younger guys in the league, nobody plays defense anymore, they all just want to be the star striker and make highlight reels for their social media. \n\nBut I guess... really, the main reason I stopped going to soccer and seeing my friends is just that I'm embarrassed. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. I don't really know if I'd say my actual worth as a human being is tied to it. I mean, I guess I haven't really thought about it in those exact terms. I don't think I'm a worthless person just because I'm struggling right now.

They force you to write these OKRs, Objectives and Key Results, every single quarter, and the interface is just an absolute nightmare. I literally had to write a custom Python script just to parse the HR data for my team's annual reviews because the vendor refused to patch a basic export bug. How do these enterprise SaaS companies get away with charging fifty bucks a seat for software that can't even handle basic string parsing? It's just... it's a scam, honestly.

We used to have ten-minute standups, you know? You'd say what you did, what you're doing, and what's blocking you, and then you get back to work. Now it's these forty-five-minute Zoom meetings where everyone just talks in circles using corporate buzzwords like \"synergy\" and \"bandwidth.\" And the video quality is always terrible because half of them are working from coffee shops on public Wi-Fi instead of a secure, hardwired connection at home. I actually had to explain to a junior developer last week why doing database migrations over a public network is a massive security risk, and he looked at me like I was speaking Greek. \n\nBut yeah, even when nothing is actually happening on the call, my leg is just bouncing a mile a minute. I just feel so restless all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or hopeless about your situation?\n\nPatient: Sad? I mean, maybe a little down sometimes, but not really hopeless. It's more just this constant, buzzing anxiety, you know? Not like a deep depression. Though, speaking of depression, I was reading this article the other day about how the tech industry has some of the highest burnout rates of any profession right now. And it makes total sense when you look at how the agile methodology has been completely bastardized by management. Agile was supposed to be about flexibility and iterative development, but now it's just an excuse for executives to change the project scope every two weeks without adjusting the budget or the timeline. They call them \"sprints,\" but it's really just a marathon where you're sprinting the entire time. I tried explaining this to a VP of Engineering at my last company, and he just gave me this blank stare and told me we needed to \"move fast and break things.\" Which is such a toxic mindset when you're dealing with enterprise-level financial data. You don't want to break things when millions of dollars are routing through your API. \n\nBut, uh, what was the question? Sadness? Yeah, no, I wouldn't say I'm hopeless. Just really, really wound up and stressed about everything falling apart.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble sleeping due to these worries?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep is... it's a disaster, honestly. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, replaying every single thing I said in a meeting, or worrying about interview answers for jobs I haven't even applied for yet. I'll just be staring at the ceiling, checking the clock at 2 a.m., then 3 a.m. \n\nWhich is exactly what used to happen when I was actually on call back in the early 2000s. We carried these physical pagers—Motorola, I think they were—and when that thing went off at 3 a.m., your heart would just drop into your stomach. Because it meant a database was corrupted or a server rack went down, and you had to drive into the data center in the middle of the night. Now, they don't even have physical pagers, they just use PagerDuty on their smartphones. And the younger guys complain about it! They complain about getting a text message that they can acknowledge from their bed while they restart a pod in Kubernetes. They have no idea what it's like to have to physically replace a hard drive in a freezing cold server room at 4 in the morning while the CEO is breathing down your neck. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, even on the rare nights where I supposedly get enough hours of sleep, I wake up feeling completely exhausted. It feels exactly like I spent the whole night on call for a massive system outage, just totally drained before the day even starts.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations or places that you've been avoiding because you fear something might go wrong or you might feel overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have been avoiding a lot, actually. Like with work, I’ve completely stopped doing my networking follow-ups, and I’m constantly delaying client deliverables. I just get completely stuck anticipating every single little thing that could go wrong with the project or the deployment. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, it is completely useless these days anyway. I’ve also pulled way back from my Sunday soccer league and just... hanging out with friends in general. Which sucks because I've known some of those guys since college. I'm embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become lately. I just snap at people over nothing, and I'm so wound up that it's just easier to stay home and avoid everyone altogether.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive, intrusive thoughts that you can't seem to get out of your head?\n\nPatient: Intrusive thoughts? I mean, I don't know if I'd call them intrusive, but my brain definitely doesn't shut off. It's just a constant stream of worrying about the same things over and over. Like, I'll just be sitting there and suddenly I'm obsessing over whether I forgot to renew the SSL certificates for the staging environment. \n\nWhich is a whole other issue, honestly. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. The way they handle certificate management now is a joke. We used to have a dedicated sysadmin who tracked all of that on a whiteboard, and you knew exactly when something was expiring. Now they rely on these automated Let's Encrypt bots that are supposed to auto-renew, but half the time the cron job fails silently and nobody knows until the site throws a security warning and the clients start screaming. And don't even get me started on the new browser security standards. Google Chrome updates its policies every five minutes, and suddenly a certificate that was perfectly fine yesterday is throwing a red flag today because they decided to deprecate some cipher suite without giving anyone a reasonable heads-up. It's impossible to keep up with, and it just creates this culture where everyone is constantly putting out fires instead of actually building solid architecture. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask? Repetitive thoughts? I guess I don't really have weird, intrusive thoughts out of nowhere. It's more just... I can't stop thinking about the stuff I'm already stressed about. It just loops and loops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these worries are coming from your own thoughts or do they feel like they're being imposed on you from the outside?\n\nPatient: No, no, they're definitely my own thoughts. It's just my own brain driving me crazy. I don't feel like anyone is beaming them into my head or anything like that. \n\nAlthough, speaking of things being imposed on you from the outside, you should see what corporate is trying to do to our department right now. They brought in this new VP of Engineering who went to some weekend leadership seminar, and now he's trying to impose this massive \"Scaled Agile Framework\" on everyone. It's a complete nightmare. You've got these guys who haven't written a line of code since 1998 suddenly dictating how our two-week sprints should be structured, and they want us using this bloated Jira setup that takes five minutes just to load a single ticket. Back when I started, we just talked to each other. We had a simple text file with a list of bugs, and we just sat down and fixed them. Now I spend half my day updating story points for middle management so they can make a colorful pie chart for the stakeholders. It's completely counterproductive and just adds to the endless pile of busywork. \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Oh, right, my thoughts. Yeah, no, nobody is putting the worries in my head. They're all mine, unfortunately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to repeat certain actions or check things over and over?\n\nPatient: Compelled to repeat things? No, not really. I don't have any weird habits like washing my hands fifty times or having to lock the door a certain number of times, if that's what you mean. \n\nI mean, I do check my email and Slack constantly, but that's just the job. Which, honestly, Slack has completely ruined the tech industry. It used to be that someone sent you an email, and you had a reasonable 24 hours to get back to them. You could actually sit down and focus on a complex problem without being interrupted. Now? If you don't reply to a message in three minutes with a little thumbs-up emoji, people think you're ignoring them or you're slacking off. And the channels! We have a channel for everything now. There's a channel for deployment, a channel for lunch orders, a channel for people's dogs... it's just endless noise. My phone buzzes in my pocket all day and half the night because some junior dev in another time zone can't figure out how to restart a Docker container, and they just tag the entire management group instead of reading the documentation. It's like we've traded actual productivity for the illusion of being constantly available, and it's exhausting. \n\nBut, uh... no. To answer your question, I don't feel forced to repeat actions or check things over and over. Just the normal checking my phone for work, but that's because I have to, not because of some compulsion.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it harder to enjoy or participate in activities you normally like because of these worries and anxieties?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it’s definitely getting harder. I mean, work-wise, I’ve been missing networking follow-ups and I've even delayed a few client deliverables lately. I just sit there and get completely stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong before I hit send. \n\nWhich, speaking of networking, those industry mixers are completely useless now anyway. It used to be you'd go, grab a beer, and actually talk to other engineers about how they solved database scaling issues or whatever. Now? It’s just a room full of twenty-something sales reps in branded Patagonia vests trying to sell you some AI-driven cloud security platform that they don't even understand. You ask them one basic technical question about their API endpoints and they just freeze and say they'll \"circle back\" with their technical lead. It's a total waste of time. I don't even know why they bother hosting these events anymore if they aren't going to send people who actually know the architecture. It's all just marketing fluff.\n\nBut, anyway... what were you asking? Oh, activities I enjoy. Yeah, it's not just work. I’ve completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I haven't been hanging out with my friends much either. I’m just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm kind of embarrassed by how I act around them now. I just snap at people over nothing. \n\nThough, to be fair, the refs in that soccer league have been absolutely blind this season. The last time I actually played, this guy on the other team was throwing elbows the entire first half, and the ref just stood there checking his watch. We pay a small fortune in league fees every season, and they can't even get someone who knows the offside rule. It’s ridiculous, you know? You go out there to blow off some steam and you just end up more frustrated than when you arrived. \n\nSo, um... yeah. I guess I'm just not doing much of anything I used to like doing right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific situations or events that trigger your worrying, or is it more of a constant, general feeling of anxiety?\n\nPatient: It’s... it’s really just constant. It’s not one specific thing setting it off, it’s everything all at once. My mind just keeps jumping from contract work to mortgage payments to whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m damaged goods. Like, if there's just a delayed email from a recruiter, that alone can feel like absolute proof that my whole career is closing down. \n\nWhich, honestly, dealing with tech recruiters right now is a complete joke anyway. They rely entirely on these automated LinkedIn scrapers. You get these messages from some 23-year-old saying, \"Hey, saw your impressive background in HTML, would you be interested in a Principal Cloud Architecture role?\" It’s insulting. They don't even know the difference between Java and JavaScript, but they hold the keys to the hiring process. And the ghosting! They’ll string you along for three weeks, demand you do a take-home coding assessment—which is ridiculous, I manage entire departments, I haven't written a sorting algorithm on a whiteboard since 2002—and then they just disappear into the void. The whole hiring ecosystem is completely broken. It's all run by Applicant Tracking Systems that throw out perfectly good resumes if you don't stuff them with the exact right buzzwords. It's just a massive waste of everyone's time.\n\nBut... sorry, what were you asking? Oh, right, if there are specific triggers. Yeah, so it's not just one trigger. It's just this constant loop in my head about the mortgage, my reputation, the contracts... it just never shuts off.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular area of your life where you feel the worrying is most prominent?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's... it's kind of everywhere. I can't really pin it down to just one area. My mind just keeps jumping from my contract work to making the mortgage payments, to... you know, worrying if people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m just damaged goods at this point. I mean, if a recruiter delays an email by even a day, it feels like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down.\n\nAnd speaking of Bethesda, the traffic getting in and out of there lately is just... it's unbelievable. They've been doing construction on 495 for what feels like a decade now. You try to get to a simple lunch meeting and you're sitting on the outer loop for 45 minutes just staring at the bumper of a Prius. They keep adding these express lanes, but they charge you like thirty dollars just to go five miles during rush hour. Who is paying for that? I mean, unless you're expensing it to a government contract, it's basically highway robbery. And don't even get me started on the Maryland drivers. They just drift across three lanes without a blinker like they're the only ones on the road. It's like nobody ever taught them basic driver's ed. \n\nBut, um... yeah, to answer your question, the worry isn't just one area. It's the work, the house, my reputation... it's all of it just constantly spinning around in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is affecting your ability to perform your job or maintain relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier, but it's definitely hitting both my work and my personal life pretty hard. With the job, I’m missing those networking follow-ups and delaying client deliverables because I just freeze up. I'll sit there staring at the screen, anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong before I actually send the files over. \n\nAnd honestly, the tools we have to use to send things out now make everything ten times worse. Microsoft Teams? What an absolute resource hog. Half the time it doesn't even sync the messages properly, so you're responding to a thread from three hours ago and looking like a complete idiot. And the notifications! It's just a constant, aggressive pinging noise. Who thought it was a good idea to build an enterprise communication platform that eats up half your RAM just to send a thumbs-up emoji? It's like the developers at Microsoft just assume everyone is running a liquid-cooled supercomputer. We actually had to upgrade the hardware for our entire tier-two support team last quarter just so they could have Teams and Chrome open at the same time without their machines grinding to a complete halt. It's terrible optimization, really. Just lazy coding.\n\nBut, um... wait, what was the second part of your question? Oh, relationships. Right. Yeah, it's affecting that too. Like I was saying before, I’ve pulled back from my Sunday soccer league and I'm barely seeing my friends anymore. I'm just so tense and irritable all the time, and I'm honestly just embarrassed by how I act around them lately. I don't want them to see me snapping at everyone over nothing, so it's easier to just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you significant distress or interfering with your ability to function on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... distress is a good word for it. It's just exhausting. Like I was saying, it's definitely messing with my ability to function. I'm missing those networking follow-ups, and I keep delaying deliverables for clients because I just sit there... paralyzed, basically, trying to anticipate every single disaster that could happen before I hit send. \n\nAnd don't even get me started on email clients these days. Outlook just pushed an update last week that completely broke the search indexing again. I mean, how many decades has Microsoft had to figure out how to search text strings in a local database? It's embarrassing. You type in a client's name and it just spins and spins, and then gives you results from 2018 that have absolutely nothing to do with what you asked for. I spend half my day just trying to find attachments I know I saved. It's like they want us to fail. The UI changes every six months just for the sake of changing, and they bury all the advanced settings under three layers of menus. It's infuriating when you're just trying to get a simple contract out the door and the software is actively fighting you.\n\nBut... sorry, yeah, the distress. It's bad. And it's not just work. I've completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I barely see my friends anymore. I'm just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm embarrassed by it. I don't want them to see me like this, snapping over every little thing, so I just... I just isolate. It's easier that way, even if it feels terrible.\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying been consistently affecting your daily life and relationships over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, consistently. It would send the text code to my phone, but by the time the server actually processed the input, the token had already expired. I spent three hours on the phone with a tier-one helpdesk guy in another timezone who just kept telling me to clear my cache and cookies. It's just been dragging on and on, affecting everything, just like I said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to your ability to succeed in your career or manage your worries?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I'd say my actual worth as a human being is tied to it. I mean... I don't know. I'm not really sure if my identity has changed. I guess I haven't really thought about it like that. I feel like the same person, just... exhausted, you know?

I think I might have mentioned this a few minutes ago, but yeah, it's been going on since I was fired, which was about thirteen months ago. And it never really let up, even after I finally found some contract work.

It's like nobody ever taught them basic driver's ed. \n\nBut, um... yeah, to answer your question, the worry isn't just one area. It's the work, the house, my reputation... it's all of it just constantly spinning around in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is affecting your ability to perform your job or maintain relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, I think I mentioned this a little bit earlier, but it's definitely hitting both my work and my personal life pretty hard. With the job, I’m missing those networking follow-ups and delaying client deliverables because I just freeze up. I'll sit there staring at the screen, anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong before I actually send the files over. \n\nAnd honestly, the tools we have to use to send things out now make everything ten times worse. Microsoft Teams? What an absolute resource hog. Half the time it doesn't even sync the messages properly, so you're responding to a thread from three hours ago and looking like a complete idiot. And the notifications! It's just a constant, aggressive pinging noise. Who thought it was a good idea to build an enterprise communication platform that eats up half your RAM just to send a thumbs-up emoji? It's like the developers at Microsoft just assume everyone is running a liquid-cooled supercomputer. We actually had to upgrade the hardware for our entire tier-two support team last quarter just so they could have Teams and Chrome open at the same time without their machines grinding to a complete halt. It's terrible optimization, really. Just lazy coding.\n\nBut, um... wait, what was the second part of your question? Oh, relationships. Right. Yeah, it's affecting that too. Like I was saying before, I’ve pulled back from my Sunday soccer league and I'm barely seeing my friends anymore. I'm just so tense and irritable all the time, and I'm honestly just embarrassed by how I act around them lately. I don't want them to see me snapping at everyone over nothing, so it's easier to just stay home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you significant distress or interfering with your ability to function on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... distress is a good word for it. It's just exhausting. Like I was saying, it's definitely messing with my ability to function. I spend half my day just trying to find attachments I know I saved. It's like they want us to fail. The UI changes every six months just for the sake of changing, and they bury all the advanced settings under three layers of menus. It's infuriating when you're just trying to get a simple contract out the door and the software is actively fighting you.\n\nBut... sorry, yeah, the distress. It's bad. And it's not just work. I've completely pulled back from my Sunday soccer league, and I barely see my friends anymore. I'm just so tense and irritable all the time, and honestly, I'm embarrassed by it. I don't want them to see me like this, snapping over every little thing, so I just... I just isolate. It's easier that way, even if it feels terrible.\n\nDoctor: Has your worrying been consistently affecting your daily life and relationships over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, consistently. Honestly, it's been a constant thing since I was let go from my old job about... I guess it's been thirteen months ago now. And it never really let up, you know? Even after I managed to string together some contract work, the worrying just stayed right there with me. \n\nSpeaking of contracts, you wouldn't believe the hoops they make you jump through just to get set up as an independent vendor these days. The onboarding portals are a nightmare. I had to use this one system last month—Workday, I think it was—and the two-factor authentication was completely broken. It would send the text code to my phone, but by the time the server actually processed the input, the token had already expired. I spent three hours on the phone with a tier-one helpdesk guy in another timezone who just kept telling me to clear my cache and cookies. Like, buddy, I manage IT systems for a living, clearing the cache isn't going to fix a latency issue with your authentication server. It's just been dragging on and on, affecting everything, just like I said.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to your ability to succeed in your career or manage your worries?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if I'd say my actual worth as a human being is tied to it. I mean, I guess I haven't really thought about it in those exact terms. I don't think I'm a worthless person just because I'm struggling right now. \n\nBut it is really hard not to feel like a total failure when you're constantly being measured by these ridiculous corporate metrics. My old company used this performance management software—I think it was Lattice, or maybe 15Five—and it was just the most demoralizing system ever built. They force you to write these OKRs, Objectives and Key Results, every single quarter, and the interface is just an absolute nightmare. You try to update your progress on a project, and the slider snaps to either 25% or 50%, there's no granular control at all. And the reporting dashboard! Completely useless. It exports to a CSV file but it strips out all the text qualifiers, so if anyone uses a simple comma in their self-evaluation, it breaks the delimiter and pushes all the data into the wrong columns in Excel. I literally had to write a custom Python script just to parse the HR data for my team's annual reviews because the vendor refused to patch a basic export bug. I feel like the same person, just... exhausted, you know? \n\nSpeaking of identities though, you wouldn't believe the identity management systems we have to deal with now. We just migrated to Okta for SSO, single sign-on, and it has been an absolute disaster. They promised seamless integration across all our cloud apps, but the SAML assertions keep failing on half our legacy platforms. So now I've got users who can log into their email but can't access the VPN, or they get into the CRM but it kicks them out every fifteen minutes because the token refresh policy is completely broken out of the box. And don't even get me started on their mobile authenticator app. It drains phone batteries like you wouldn't believe. I've had executives screaming at me because their iPhones are dying by noon just because this app is constantly polling the server in the background. And it never really let up, even after I finally found some contract work. \n\nActually, speaking of getting fired, the way they handled my IT offboarding that day was just completely absurd. I was literally still on the exit interview call with HR when my screen just went black. They had triggered a remote wipe using Microsoft Intune, but whoever configured the policy didn't set the exclusions right. Instead of just wiping the corporate data container, it initiated a full cryptographic erase of the entire SSD. I mean, I set up that tenant originally, and I specifically documented that you only do a full wipe for lost or stolen devices! Because when you do a full wipe, it scrambles the BitLocker keys, and unless the device has a stable connection to Azure AD to escrow the new keys—which it didn't, because the script also immediately killed the Wi-Fi profile—you basically turn a two-thousand-dollar laptop into a paperweight. They actually had to email my personal account the next day to ask me how to unlock the BIOS because their tier-two guys couldn't figure it out. It's just... the sheer incompetence is staggering. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I'm rambling again. Yeah, thirteen months. That's how long it's been."
